brain_volumetry

Verlaufs-Volumetrie: Trendseite plus Fehlerseite (Test-Retest, SDC95, ICC, Nachweisbarkeit, Ausfall/QC)

Volumetrie, SynthSeg, FreeSurfer, Atrophie, Laengsschnitt, Test-Retest, SDC, ICC, Messfehler, Hippocampus, MRT

18 vorschlag 15 getragen

Volumetrie des Gehirns ueber die Zeit (SynthSeg, FreeSurfer, eigene Netze): wie Verlaufszahlen gerechnet und wie ihre Messfehler ausgewiesen werden. Keine Patientendaten; Zahlen bleiben bei den Projekten.

Stand 2026-10-13. Ein Eintrag je Datei.

Kurzstand

Neu 2026-10-30 (eigene Laeufe): Tiefe graue Substanz skaliert in DLBS nur mit ICV^0,65-0,85 (Kortex ~1), Division ueberkorrigiert grosse Koepfe -> ICV als Kovariate (icv-scaling-exponent-and-asymmetry-sdc-2026-10); Thalamus-Asymmetrie SDC95 ~5 pp, Scannerstufe hebt sich in der Asymmetrie auf. Regel erweitert: der read-Fehler liess erfolgreiche recon-all-Jobs still auf der FLAIR laufen (one-t1-per-visit-and-tab-columns-by-cut).

Neu 2026-10-19 (zwei Literaturberichte, 65 Quellen geprueft, 0 falsch): SynthSeg 2 in FreeSurfer 8.2 ist fuer rohe T1 gebaut (Dice 0,88 gegen Hand); QC-Schwelle 0,65 ist fuer die Ganzscan-Entscheidung validiert (Sens. 0,999, Spez. 0,906, AUC 0,998, 594 klinische Scans), je Struktur nur angewendet; Laengsschnitt-Stream 15-50 % praeziser; Thalamus, Hippocampus, Amygdala, Pallidum und alle Veraenderungen mit zweiter Methode bestaetigen; FS 7.x und 8.x nie mischen, Build und Betriebssystem zwischen Workstation und HPC festhalten (research-synthseg2-freesurfer82-validation-2026-10).

Neu 2026-10-18 (alle 16 Literaturfragen zweimal unabhaengig recherchiert, 512 Quellen geprueft, 0 falsch): Einig: Scannerwechsel als zeitabhaengiger Term, ICV als Kovariate, Laengsschnitt-Weg praeziser, QC als Kaskade. Uneinig: Spiegelfuellung (keine volumetrische Validierung gefunden; FSL lesion_filling schwaechster Fueller), Roh- oder gefuelltes T1 primaer, ICV je Sitzung, longComBat vs Scanner-Term, fruehes Intervall, WMH-Werkzeug (research-first-vs-second-run-volumetry-2026-10).

Neu 2026-10-18 (vier Literaturberichte, 91 Quellen geprueft, plus eine Regel): Kopfgroesse einmal je Person schaetzen, Strukturen im Infarkt markieren (research-icv-normalisation-and-lesioned-structures-2026-10); Ausfall ist informativ, Tod und Ausstieg getrennt modellieren (research-attrition-and-missing-data-longitudinal-stroke-2026-10); Scannerwechsel (z. B. Trio -> Prisma, Software-Update, Spule) verschiebt Volumen um mehrere Prozent und muss als zeitabhaengige Kovariate oder per longComBat behandelt werden (research-scanner-change-within-subject-longitudinal-2026-10); STROBE + COBIDAS als Berichtsgeruest (research-methods-reporting-checklist-multisite-longitudinal-mri-2026-10). Regel: SynthSeg --parc hat keine Labels 3/42 (synthseg-parc-moves-cortex-labels).

Neu 2026-10-18 (Literaturbericht, 32 Quellen geprueft): Querschnitts-Segmentierung pro Sitzung (SynthSeg/recon-all) reicht fuer 5-Jahres-Atrophie innerhalb einer Person nicht; der FreeSurfer-Laengsschnitt-Stream ist systematisch praeziser (+15-50 %), der Rest ist Rauschen (research-cross-sectional-vs-longitudinal-consistency-2026-10). Kernpunkte: SDC-basiertes Flagging implausibler Volumenanstiege (Ganzhirn ~0,6-1,1 %, je Struktur 1-15 % innerhalb / 5-29 % zwischen Scannern); unbiasiertes Within-Subject-Template als Kernkorrektur; Laesionswachstum verzerrt GM/Dicke, Fuellen ist Standard; longComBat fuer unser Design, aber nur bei nachgewiesenem Scanner-Effekt.

Neu 2026-10-18 (drei Literaturberichte, geprueft): Kortexfehler ~38 % in klinischen T1 sind erwartbar, Euler-Zahl ist die beste billige QC-Kennzahl, Verlaeufe mit gemischten Modellen + longComBat (research-cortex-segmentation-multisite-older-stroke-2026-10, research-segmentation-qc-at-scale-2026-10, research-longitudinal-volumetry-statistics-after-stroke-2026-10).

Neu 2026-10-18 (Literaturbericht, 21 Quellen geprueft): Wie die Qualitaet automatischer Segmentierungen in einer Kohorte von ~1500 Sitzungen zu steuern ist (literature-segmentation-qc-at-cohort-scale-2026-10). Kernpunkte: kein Einzelwert entscheidet; Euler-Zahl bester belegter Skalar (AUC 0,98-0,99), MRIQC/CAT12 ohne Anpassung nur kappa 0,30/0,28; Volumen-Ausreisser nie als Ausschlusskriterium (erhoehte unerklaerte Varianz); SynthSeg-QC 36 % -> 91 % nach Rescale/Crop; bewertete Stichprobe ueber kappa-KI dimensionieren; Edits brauchen eine Kovariate und eine Sensitivitaetsanalyse.

Neu 2026-10-17 (drei Literaturberichte, je Quelle geprueft und zusammengefasst): Fraktale Dimensionalitaet zuverlaessig (ICC ~0,9-0,95), aber standort-/registrierungsabhaengig (research-fractal-dimensionality-from-freesurfer-2026-10); Fuellen statt Ausblenden, Artefakte wachsen mit Last (research-lesion-filling-before-segmentation-2026-10); subkortikal entscheiden Laengsschnitt-Template und longComBat, Scanner-SDC 4,8-29 % (research-subcortical-segmentation-longitudinal-stroke-2026-10).

Neu 2026-10-16: Sequenzen derselben Sitzung (FLAIR auf T2*/SWI) per Header-Weltkoordinaten zusammenbringen; ein FLIRT-Start im Voxelraum drehte LPS- gegen RAS-Dateien falsch. SynthSeg aus FLAIR nur, wo es die Hirnmaske abdeckt (same-session-sequences-align-by-header-not-voxel-flirt).

Stand 2026-10-13. Prefill fuer Chats und Agenten. Details in INDEX.md.

Verlaufs-Volumetrie liefert zwei Seiten: die Trendseite (gemischtes Modell, Steigung je Proband, Seitenvergleich) und die Fehlerseite. Die Fehlerseite hat vier Bausteine: Test-Retest je Struktur aus zwei Segmentierungen derselben Sitzung (SDw, CV, ICC(2,1), SDC95 = 1,96 * sqrt 2 * SDw, Bland-Altman), Laengsschnitt-Residuen und kuerzestes Intervall, Nachweisbarkeit (Jahre bis eine Person die SDC ueberschreitet, KI in denselben Einheiten), Ausfall/QC je Zeitpunkt. Laesionsausschluss nur, wenn die Maske in allen Sitzungen existiert, sonst entstehen Maskeneffekte von +1400 %.

Lesen bei: Volumetrie, SynthSeg, FreeSurfer, Atrophie, Verlauf, Laengsschnitt, Test-Retest, SDC, ICC, Messfehler, Hippocampus.

Neu 2026-10-12: SynthSeg in FreeSurfer 8.2 rechnet nur auf CPU (85 s je Sitzung mit --robust); Vollkohorten in Ordner-Batches, 3 parallel. Nach jeder Kohortenkopie gzip -t ueber alle NIfTI (eine abgeschnittene T1 lag 5 Wochen unbemerkt). Rechenplan-Literatur: FreeSurfer longitudinal stream (Reuter 2012) und Euler-Zahl als QC-Gate (Rosen 2018) sind die belegten Hebel; FastSurfer-Dicke ICC 0,92 vs 0,87.

Neu 2026-10-13: recon-all 8.2 braucht seine 26 GB nur in den ersten ~15 Minuten (SynthSeg/SynthMorph), danach 2-4 GB; wer ganze Laeufe statt dieser Phase begrenzt, laesst die halbe CPU brach (gemessen: 3,4 Sitzungen je Stunde bei MAX_P=2, 174 OOM-Kills bei MAX_P=5). Vorschlag: Schleuse nur um autorecon1 (~7,5 je Stunde, DEMDAS-Vollkohorte ~9 statt ~20 Tage); auf Slurm je Sitzung 36 GB, 4 CPU, 6 h. Example-Set: 61/65 Sitzungen mit Thickness fertig (13.10. 00:01).

Neu 2026-10-13 (mit Quellen): FreeSurfer-Plan fuer Schlaganfall-Laengsschnitte: Long-Stream statt Einzelsitzungen (Vidal-Pineiro 2025: +0,25 ICC; 0-6 Monate ist Rauschen, Raten aus 0/36/60), Laesionen vor recon-all fuellen (Chard 2010, Battaglini 2012, Magon 2014; ONDRI Ozzoude 2020: Fehlrate 62 % -> 8 %), Euler-Zahl als QC-Gate (Rosen 2018, Monereo-Sanchez 2021), eigene SDC95 aus 40 Doppelaufnahmen, longComBat (Beer 2020). Jeder Wiki-Eintrag nennt ab jetzt seine Quellen (Nutzerregel 13.10.).

Neu 2026-10-13 (nachmittags, mit Quellen): Durchsicht aller 17 Mehrfach-Sitzungs-Probanden: Ausrichtung und Serienregistrierung ueberall gut; chronische Infarkte bei Folgesitzungen ohne Maske (DWI-Modell nur akut), SynthSeg gibt Hoehlen Label 0 -> vorwaerts getragene Maske noetig. Euler-Zahl nur aus dem Binary (LD_LIBRARY_PATH), nie aus dem Log-Ende. Protokoll v1 eingefroren. HPC-Messung: SynthSeg robust 50 s / 19,9 GB je Sitzung, SAMSEG 7 min / 11 GB.

Einträge aufklappen: Klick auf die Zeile. Alle öffnen / schließen.

Regel (6)

RegelOne T1 per visit, and TSV columns by `cut`, never by `read`getragen2026-10-30HPC-Pilot: `read` verschluckt leere TSV-Spalten, zwei T1 je Besuch unter einer id; 5 von 14 "FAILED" waren Listenfehler, FLAIRpial-Abbruch 3/65 ist Befund

Herkunft: DEMDAS HPC pilot (65 sessions, FreeSurfer 8.1.0), 2026-10-13 · eigene Seite · Markdown

Aussage

Two list bugs produced 5 of 14 "failed" recon-all jobs in the DEMDAS HPC pilot (Slurm arrays 11179269/70, 13.10.2026):

  1. Empty TSV columns vanish with read. IFS=$'\t' read -r a b c ... collapses consecutive tabs, so a row with an empty run or pair field shifts every later column: recon-all received the DWI path as -i ("ERROR: cannot find ...dwi..."; array ids 49, 57, 64). Rule: parse each column with cut -f N (keeps empty fields) or in Python with csv.
  2. Two T1 files of the same visit under one id. A DEMDAS session can hold the distortion-corrected MPRAGE (run-2_T1w) and the uncorrected ND copy (_T1w, no run). The list treated them as two rows with the same id, so two jobs wrote into one working directory and destroyed each other (MRIsplineNormalize(): npeaks = 1, line-search failure). On the HPC copy only the ND file existed for the two sessions, and recon-all 8.1.0 failed on the ND image while Alita (8.2.0, run-2) succeeded. Rule: a "pair" is only run-1 + run-2 (two acquisitions); otherwise take exactly one T1, run-2 preferred, as gm_beispiel.py does.

A third failure class is a finding, not a bug: -FLAIRpial dies with "Numerical result out of range" in the vertex loop of mris_place_surface for 3 of 65 sessions whose base run succeeds (array ids 10, 44, 47). The base variant is kept for those.

Update 2026-10-30: the jobs that "succeeded" were wrong too. The same read shift hit every row whose run field was empty, but only the rows where the shifted path was a DWI crashed. Where the shifted path was the FLAIR, recon-all ran to the end on the FLAIR as its T1 input: 49 of 50 kept pilot sessions (FS 8.1.0) show mri_convert ..._FLAIR.nii.gz in scripts/recon-all.log. Nothing failed; the outputs simply looked like recon-all outputs. It surfaced two weeks later in a method check: cortex about 28 % smaller than the T1-based run of the same session (FS 8.2.0), and Euler numbers of the two runs uncorrelated (rho -0.19). Two rules follow:

  1. After fixing an input-parsing bug, audit the jobs that succeeded, not only the ones that failed. Grep the input actually used (mri_convert line in recon-all.log, -i in the command) for every finished job.
  2. A resumable array skips finished outputs, including wrong ones. The fixed script tests surf/rh.thickness and exits "done", so the bad runs stay in place, and the longitudinal stream (-base/-long) picks them up as time points. Delete wrong outputs explicitly before re-submitting, and rebuild every template that used them.

Trägt

  • Local re-run of both list scripts on the 24-participant example set: old 115 rows with 57 duplicate ids, new 58 rows with 0 duplicates; full cohort unchanged (1528 rows, 0 duplicates).
  • hpc/make_session_list.py, hpc_py/01_make_session_list.py, all four *.sbatch patched; copies on the stick (HPC_SCRIPTS, HPC_SCRIPTS_PY) and transfer folder HPC_TRANSFER with the two run-2 T1.

Trägt nicht

  • Whether recon-all 8.2.0 also fails on the ND image is untested (the ND copy is only a fallback).

Bruchindikator

  • Any recon-all.log whose first mri_convert input is not a *_T1w.nii.gz.
  • A session id occurring twice in lists/sessions.tsv, or -i pointing outside anat/.

Ablesung

  • 2026-10-30: own check, FS 8.1.0 vs 8.2.0 on 47 shared sessions; input audit of 50 pilot recon-all.log files (Tier: own data).
  • Slurm logs log/reconall_<array>_<idx>.out, sacct (FAILED 1:0, 52-88 min), user-pasted terminal output 13.10.2026 (Tier: own data).
  • FreeSurfer recon-all documentation on -FLAIRpial (https://surfer.nmr.mgh.harvard.edu/fswiki/recon-all), FreeSurfer 8.1.0 module on the Charité HPC.

Verknüpft

  • demdas-pipeline-protocol-v1
RegelSynthSeg with --parc has no labels 3/42: the cortex is 1001-1035 / 2001-2035getragen2026-10-18SynthSeg --parc has no labels 3/42: count the cortex as 1001-1035/2001-2035; check label lists against a real segmentation

Herkunft: own pipeline audit 2026-10-18 (Alita); two own scripts of 2026-10-17 counted the cortex with labels 3/42 only · eigene Seite · Markdown

Aussage

mri_synthseg --parc (FreeSurfer 8.2.0, SynthSeg 2) replaces Left/Right-Cerebral-Cortex (FreeSurfer LUT 3/42) by the Desikan-Killiany parcels 1001-1035 and 2001-2035. Code that selects the cortex as [3, 42] silently gets zero voxels: on 2026-10-17 a cohort volume table had cortex = 0 ml in every session (so no cortex growth flag could ever fire), and a rating page drew no cortex outline on any of its images, which also made a vision model's "no cortex error" verdicts meaningless. Rule: cortex = [3, 42] + range(1000, 1036) + range(2000, 2036), and every new label list is checked against np.unique of one real segmentation before a cohort run (zero-voxel classes are an error, not a result).

Trägt

Own audit 2026-10-18: after the fix the outline appears on every image (orange pixels per image from ~3 to ~44 000) and the cortex volume column is non-zero. Label definitions: FreeSurferColorLUT; Billot et al. 2023, PNAS, "Robust machine learning segmentation for large-scale analysis of heterogeneous clinical brain MRI datasets", doi:10.1073/pnas.2216399120, PMID 36802420 (SynthSeg 2 with cortical parcellation).

Trägt nicht

Without --parc the labels 3/42 exist; code must handle both.

Bruchindikator

A structure total of exactly 0 in every row of a table, or an overlay colour that never appears in the rendered images.

Ablesung

2026-10-18, pipeline audit of a ~1500-session cohort; pixel count of the overlay colour per image, np.unique of a segmentation.

RegelTake the Euler number from `mris_euler_number` on `?h.orig.nofix`; a log fallback can return the post-fix value 2getragen2026-10-13mris_euler_number auf orig.nofix mit LD_LIBRARY_PATH; Log-Fallback liefert Nach-Fix-Wert 2

Herkunft: QC gate of the DEMDAS pilot, 13.10.2026: 6 of 61 finished sessions reported Euler = 2 · eigene Seite · Markdown

Aussage

The QC-relevant Euler number is that of the UNCORRECTED surface (surf/lh.orig.nofix, rh.orig.nofix), read with mris_euler_number. In FreeSurfer 8.2 the binary needs the ITK libraries (libitkvnl_algo-5.3.so.1) on LD_LIBRARY_PATH; without them it fails silently in a subprocess and a fallback that greps recon-all.log can pick the LAST "euler #" line, which is the topology-fixed surface (always 2). In the pilot this produced 6 sessions with Euler 2 and a wrong "recon-all pending" state; with the library path set, all 61 sessions had values between -142 and -15, cohort cutoff Q1 - 1.5 IQR = -109.5, no session flagged.

Traegt

Every QC gate built on the Euler number (Rosen 2018); every tool that wraps FreeSurfer 8 binaries from a conda Python.

Traegt nicht

FreeSurfer installations whose binaries find their libraries on the default path.

Bruchindikator

An Euler number of exactly 2 or an empty value for a finished session.

Ablesung

13.10.2026 14:20-14:35, qc_gate.py before/after the LD_LIBRARY_PATH fix (61 sessions).

Quellen

  • Rosen AFG et al. 2018. Quantitative assessment of structural image quality. NeuroImage 169:407-418. DOI 10.1016/j.neuroimage.2017.12.059. PMID 29278774.
  • Monereo-Sanchez J et al. 2021. Quality control strategies for brain MRI segmentation and parcellation: insights from the Maastricht Study. NeuroImage 237:118174. DOI 10.1016/j.neuroimage.2021.118174. PMID 34000406.

Verknuepft

  • freesurfer-improvement-plan-for-a-longitudinal-stroke-cohort
RegelSchedule recon-all 8 by phase, not by runvorschlag2026-10-13Vorschlag: Schleuse nur um autorecon1 (Synth-Phase), autorecon2/3 frei -> ~7,5 statt 3,4 Sitzungen je Stunde; getragen erst nach dem ersten gemessenen Batch

Herkunft: derived from the recon-all 8.2 phase profile on Alita, 13.10.2026; not yet run as a batch · eigene Seite · Markdown

Aussage

On a single machine, gate recon-all 8.x on the memory-heavy Synth phase (first ~15 min, 26 GB resident), not on the number of whole runs. Pattern: recon-all -autorecon1 behind a semaphore of floor((RAM - 10 GB) / 28 GB) slots, then -autorecon2 -autorecon3 unrestricted up to cores / threads. Expected gain on a 24-core, 62 GB box: 3.4 to ~7.5 sessions per hour (20 to ~9 days for 1616 DEMDAS sessions), same software, same numbers. On a cluster, submit one session per array task with the Synth-phase memory as the request (36 GB) and let the scheduler pack.

Trägt

  • FreeSurfer 8.0 to 8.2 recon-all on CPU; any T1w cohort large enough that days matter.
  • Resume semantics stay the same (lh/rh.thickness as the done criterion, stale done-marker removed).

Trägt nicht

  • recon-all 7.x (no heavy phase), FastSurfer (GPU segmentation), machines with RAM >= runs x 28 GB (gate is moot).
  • Longitudinal stream (-long): base and long steps have their own profile, measure first.

Bruchindikator

  • A batch with the phase gate that still produces OOM kills or no throughput gain over MAX_P=2 on the same box.
  • Status moves to "getragen" after the first full batch with measured throughput; to "gebrochen" if the gain is < 1.5x.

Ablesung

blind until the first phase-gated batch runs; profile in the linked finding.

Verknüpft

recon-all-8-memory-peak-is-a-15-minute-phase

RegelAfter copying a cohort, run `gzip -t` over every NIfTI and compare file counts and byte totals with the sourcegetragen2026-10-12nach jeder Kohortenkopie gzip -t ueber alle NIfTI, Dateizahl und Bytes gegen die Quelle; du auf exFAT luegt

Herkunft: DEMDAS pilot, 12.10.2026: one T1w was a truncated copy (508 KB of 5.2 MB) since 03.09. · eigene Seite · Markdown

Aussage

A truncated .nii.gz is silent until a tool reads past the cut: in the DEMDAS pilot one T1w (508 KB instead of 5.2 MB) sat in the BIDS tree for five weeks; SynthSeg failed with zlib.error: invalid distance code deep in a log, the session simply vanished from the QC page ("31 of 63"), and recon-all would have failed the same way. The sweep is cheap: 886 NIfTI (12 GB) in under two minutes. For a cohort copy, also compare file count and summed bytes of source and target, and run an rsync -c dry run; du -sh is useless on exFAT (cluster inflation: 67 GB shown for 43.7 GB of data).

Traegt

Every copy of imaging data from USB drives, network shares or sticks; the inventory step of a new cohort.

Traegt nicht

Uncompressed files; corruption inside a valid gzip stream (that needs a hash from the source).

Bruchindikator

A session missing from a QC page without a stated reason; a .nii.gz far smaller than its siblings.

Ablesung

12.10.2026 16:16-16:25 (gzip sweep, inventory size 5.221 MB vs local 508 KB, replacement from the external SSD).

Verknuepft

  • synthseg-in-freesurfer-8-runs-on-cpu-only
RegelJede Verlaufs-Volumetrie bekommt eine Fehlerstatistik neben der Trendstatistikgetragen2026-10-12Zu jeder Trendseite eine Fehlerseite: Test-Retest (SDw, CV, ICC, SDC95, Bland-Altman), Laengsschnitt-Residuen, Nachweisbarkeit (Jahre bis SDC), Ausfall/QC je Zeitpunkt; Laesionsausschluss nur mit Maske in allen Sitzungen

Herkunft: Nutzer 12.10.2026 zum DEMDAS-GM-Beispiel: "bei DEMDAS muss man auch die Fehlerstatistiken rechnen"; umgesetzt in demdas-gm/fehler_statistik.py · eigene Seite · Markdown

Aussage

Eine Seite mit jaehrlichen Aenderungen (gemischtes Modell, Steigungen je Proband) ist ohne Messfehler nicht lesbar: niemand kann sehen, ob -0,8 %/Jahr Amygdala mehr ist als das Rauschen. Deshalb gehoert zu jeder Verlaufs-Volumetrie eine zweite Seite error_statistics.html mit vier Bausteinen, alle ohne neue Bildrechnung, wenn die Daten da sind:

  1. Test-Retest je Struktur aus zwei Segmentierungen derselben Sitzung (hier: MPRAGE ohne und mit Verzeichnungskorrektur, beide SynthSeg). Kennzahlen: Mittel, Bias, SD der Differenz, Within-Subject-SD = SD/sqrt 2, CV %, ICC(2,1), SDC95 = 1,96 * sqrt 2 * SDw (kleinste Aenderung, die bei EINER Person als echt gelten darf), Bland-Altman-Plots. Ehrlich kennzeichnen, wenn das Paar kein echtes Scan-Rescan ist: dann ist es eine obere Schranke.
  2. Laengsschnitt-Rauschen aus der Pipeline selbst: Residuen-SD um die Gerade je Proband (>= 3 Sitzungen, % der Baseline) und die Aenderung ueber das kuerzeste Intervall (M0 -> M6) als Verteilung. Enthaelt Biologie + Fehler, sagt das auch.
  3. Nachweisbarkeit: jaehrliche Aenderung (gemischtes Modell, mit KI in denselben Einheiten!) gegen SDC95 -> Jahre, bis eine Einzelperson die SDC ueberschreitet. Ein Balkenbild mit beiden Groessen je Struktur.
  4. Ausfall- und QC-Zaehlung je Zeitpunkt: Sitzungen, Segmentierungs-QC unter Schwelle, fehlende Laesionsmasken, Ausrichtungs-Dice, recon-all fertig/abgebrochen, Zusatzgroessen (WMH) vorhanden.

Nebenregel aus dem ersten Lauf: relative Aenderungen mit Laesionsausschluss nur, wenn die Maske in allen Sitzungen existiert. Fehlt sie in Folgesitzungen, werden aus einem fast weggemaskten M0-Wert Aenderungen von +1400 % -- ein Maskeneffekt, der in jeder Verlaufszahl steckt, bis die chronische Maske da ist.

Traegt

Jede Verlaufs-Volumetrie (SynthSeg, FreeSurfer, eigene Netze) auf dem Stick oder fuer Dritte; die Deutung jeder Steigung; die Entscheidung, ob ein Laengsschnitt-Strom (FreeSurfer longitudinal) noetig ist (ja, wenn Jahre bis SDC > Studiendauer).

Traegt nicht

Querschnittsvergleiche zwischen Gruppen (dort zaehlt die Gruppen-SD, nicht die SDC); Datensaetze ohne zweite Segmentierung je Sitzung -- dann bleibt nur Baustein 2 bis 4 und die Literatur-SDC.

Bruchindikator

Eine Trendseite ohne Verweis auf die Fehlerseite; eine Nachweisbarkeitstabelle, deren KI in anderen Einheiten steht als die Punktschaetzung (im ersten Lauf passiert: %ICV/Jahr neben relativ %/Jahr).

Ablesung

Umgesetzt 12.10.2026 (demdas-gm/fehler_statistik.py, 61 Sitzungspaare, Seite in gm_beispiel/verlauf/). Zahlen bleiben unter ~/data (eigene Kohorte). Offen: ND-Serie mit den Pipeline-Einstellungen (--parc --robust) nachrechnen, damit Baustein 1 exakt der Pipeline entspricht und die Rindenregionen dazukommen.

Verknuepft

  • qc-pages-explain-every-number-on-hover

Befund (27)

BefundDeep grey matter does not scale 1:1 with ICV; asymmetry is the scanner-robust endpointvorschlag2026-10-30DLBS: deep GM scales with ICV^0.65-0.85 (cortex ~1), so division over-corrects; asymmetry SDC95 ~5 pp, scanner step cancels in the asymmetry (2026-10-30)

Herkunft: own runs 2026-10-29/30 (DLBS public data; own stroke cohort as relative values), SynthSeg 2.0 --robust in FreeSurfer 8.2.0 · eigene Seite · Markdown

Aussage

  1. ICV scaling exponent (DLBS, healthy, wave 1, MMSE >= 26, n = 464). In log(volume) = a + b log(ICV) + age + age^2 + sex, cortex b = 0.99 [0.95; 1.02] and total GM b = 0.97 [0.93; 1.00], but deep GM is clearly below 1: thalamus 0.76 [0.68; 0.83], caudate 0.74, putamen 0.81, amygdala 0.84, pallidum 0.85, hippocampus 0.65 [0.56; 0.75]; cerebral WM 1.13. Dividing by ICV therefore over-corrects large heads: with men's ICV about 14 % larger, a man's thalamus fraction is about 3 % (hippocampus 4.5 %) lower from head size alone. Rule: ICV as a covariate (or volume / ICV^b) for deep GM; plain fractions only for cortex and total GM.
  2. Measurement error of a left-right asymmetry (own cohort, same-session repeat T1, QC-passed pairs). SDC95 of the thalamus asymmetry index is about 5 percentage points, similar to the bilateral thalamus SDC95 (about 5 %); the left pallidum is unstable (SDC95 about 20 %).
  3. A scanner change shifts every volume (about +1 to +2 %, structure-specific, pallidum about twice) but cancels in the asymmetry (thalamus asymmetry step -0.9 pp, CI includes 0), and adds no extra spread beyond the step.

Trägt

  • Bootstrap CIs over participants (2000 draws); QC-filtered sample (SynthSeg QC >= 0.65) gives the same exponents within 0.01.
  • Scanner step confirmed within the sites that changed scanners, not only across sites.
  • Fits the literature recommendation of ICV as a covariate over division (see verweist_auf).

Trägt nicht

  • Only 18 QC-passed same-session pairs: the SDC CIs are wide (thalamus asymmetry about 3-6.5 pp).
  • Exponents from one scanner model (DLBS, Philips 3T MPRAGE); other scanners not tested.
  • The SynthSeg QC score is not a lesion-misclassification detector, so QC-passed does not mean lesion-correct.

Bruchindikator

  • A structure/ICV fraction that still correlates with ICV within sex (r clearly away from 0) is being used for group comparisons.
  • Without the QC filter a few failed segmentations dominate the SD (ICV SDC95 rose about 20-fold): use a robust (MAD) SD or the QC filter.

Ablesung

  • DLBS: Dallas Lifespan Brain Study, OpenNeuro ds004856 (CC0), https://openneuro.org/datasets/ds004856 (accessed 2026-10).
  • SynthSeg: Billot B et al. 2023, PNAS, DOI 10.1073/pnas.2216399120, PMID 36802420; FreeSurfer 8.2.0, https://surfer.nmr.mgh.harvard.edu.
  • SDC definition 1.96 x sqrt(2) x within-subject SD; own runs 2026-10-29 (scaling) and 2026-10-29/30 (SDC, scanner step).

Verknüpft

BefundLiterature: how good is SynthSeg 2 in FreeSurfer 8.2 (`mri_synthseg --parc --robust`) for longitudinal volumetryvorschlag2026-10-19Literature (2 reports, 65 refs, checked): accuracy, precision, QC threshold 0.65 and version effects of SynthSeg 2 / FreeSurfer 8.2

Herkunft: two independent research reports by the remote-model agent (Clara on the hospital model hub), checked by Claude Code 2026-10-19 (quellen-pruefen 65 refs, 0 wrong; QC-threshold claim checked against the full text of Billot 2023 PNAS) · eigene Seite · Markdown

Aussage

  • Accuracy: SynthSeg reaches mean Dice 0.88 against manual labels on 1 mm MPRAGE (supervised in-domain network 0.91, SAMSEG 0.85) and is designed for raw scans without skull stripping or bias correction [1]. The robust version with cortical parcellation, QC and ICV ("SynthSeg+") is what --parc --robust runs [2]. Thin structures (cortex) lose accuracy first; no per-parcel validation of labels 1001-1035/2001-2035 against manual tracing was found.
  • QC threshold 0.65 is validated for the whole-scan decision: "reject if at least one region scores below 0.65" gave sensitivity 0.999, specificity 0.906, accuracy 0.981, AUC 0.998 against an expert's pass/fail on 594 clinical scans; the value was chosen on a validation set. Per structure the authors applied it (age trajectories) but did not validate it separately [2]. The two reports disagreed here; the full text settles it.
  • Precision: within-scanner smallest detectable change in a six-package comparison ranged from about 1 % (whole brain) upwards, and every package showed systematic between-scanner differences (between-scanner SDC from 4.8 %, SynthSeg thalamus) [3]. A same-session test-retest in an own cohort gave SDC95 0.66 % (brain), 1.38 % (cortex), but 4.67 % for the thalamus, worse than the 2-3 % reported for SynthSeg and SAMSEG in [3] (full-text value, per review).
  • The FreeSurfer longitudinal stream improves test-retest precision by 15-50 % over cross-sectional processing [4]; per-session SynthSeg is cross-sectional, so change measures need recon-all -long or SAMSEG for confirmation.
  • Versions: recon-all 8.0 replaced the front end with SynthSeg, SynthStrip and SynthMorph and gives different output than version 7; 8.1 and 8.2 give the same output as 8.0 (FreeSurfer release notes, documentation, not peer-reviewed) [D1]. Never mix 7.x and 8.x within one longitudinal series. The 8.2 notes also warn that different combinations of build and execution operating system can give different results, and offer fs-allowed-os.txt / fs-allowed-versions.txt to lock them [D1]: workstation and HPC runs of the same series must use the same build and OS, or be compared first.
  • Scanner change: a major upgrade shifted whole-brain volume by 3.4 % in healthy controls; a fixed corrective term was estimated [5].
  • Lesions: in ischaemic stroke, masking infarcts before automated segmentation made hippocampal volumes agree less with manual tracing [6]; evidence on lesion filling is mixed, so keep the unfilled segmentation as a sensitivity analysis.
  • Trust at group level: ICV, brain, GM, WM, ventricles, total cortex, thalamus. Confirm with a second method: hippocampus, amygdala, pallidum, thalamus and all change measures.

Trägt

Two independent reports (35 + 32 references; quellen-pruefen 65 PMIDs correct, 0 wrong, 2 preprint/documentation items without PMID). Threshold numbers read in the full text of [2] (Table 1 and Methods).

Trägt nicht

Most precision figures come from MS or healthy cohorts, not stroke; the thalamus value from [3] was not in its abstract. No peer-reviewed validation of recon-all 8.2 itself was found (only documentation). Pallidum reliability for SynthSeg specifically was not retrievable.

Bruchindikator

A per-structure validation of the 0.65 threshold showing poor agreement with visual ratings, or a validation of FreeSurfer 8.x showing version effects within 8.x.

Ablesung

Sources: 1. Billot B, Greve DN, Puonti O, et al. 2023. SynthSeg: segmentation of brain MRI scans of any contrast and resolution without retraining. Med Image Anal 86:102789. DOI 10.1016/j.media.2023.102789. PMID 36857946. 2. Billot B, Magdamo C, Cheng Y, et al. 2023. Robust machine learning segmentation for large-scale analysis of heterogeneous clinical brain MRI datasets. PNAS 120:e2216399120. DOI 10.1073/pnas.2216399120. PMID 36802420. 3. van Nederpelt DR, Amiri H, Brouwer I, et al. 2023. Reliability of brain atrophy measurements in multiple sclerosis using MRI: an assessment of six freely available software packages for cross-sectional analyses. Neuroradiology 65:1459-1472. DOI 10.1007/s00234-023-03189-8. PMID 37526657. 4. Rusinek H, Bokacheva L, Chen H, et al. 2026. Test-retest reliability of FreeSurfer measures of neurodegeneration. NeuroImage 333:121920. DOI 10.1016/j.neuroimage.2026.121920. PMID 41966233. 5. Sinnecker T, Schadelin S, Benkert P, et al. 2022. Brain atrophy measurement over a MRI scanner change in multiple sclerosis. NeuroImage Clin 36:103148. DOI 10.1016/j.nicl.2022.103148. PMID 36007437. 6. Khlif MS, Egorova N, Werden E, et al. 2019. A comparison of automated segmentation and manual tracing in estimating hippocampal volume in ischemic stroke and healthy control participants. NeuroImage Clin 21:101581. DOI 10.1016/j.nicl.2018.10.019. PMID 30606656. D1. FreeSurfer release notes 8.0.0-8.2.0, https://surfer.nmr.mgh.harvard.edu/fswiki/ReleaseNotes (documentation).

BefundLiterature: how to control automatic segmentation quality in a cohort of ~1500 sessionsvorschlag2026-10-18Literature (21 refs, checked): no single score decides QC; Euler number best validated scalar (AUC 0.98-0.99); MRIQC/CAT12 kappa 0.30/0.28 off the shelf; never trim on volume outliers; SynthSeg QC 36% -> 91% after rescale/crop; size the rated subsample via kappa CI; edits need a covariate

Herkunft: research report by the remote-model agent (Clara on the hospital model hub) with PubMed/Europe PMC retrieval, /home/uchralt/cle-arbeit/recherche/g_segmentation_qc_at_scale/REPORT.md, quellen-pruefen 0 wrong (21 refs) · eigene Seite · Markdown

Aussage

  • There is exactly one head-to-head comparison of QC strategies at our scale: 12 strategies (2 manual, 5 automated: MRIQC, Qoala-T, global morphometry, Euler number, CNR; 5 semi-automated) applied to 259 FreeSurfer-processed scans, judged by the reduction of unexplained variance. Manual QC won; Euler number and MRIQC were the best automated substitutes; exclusion on global morphological outliers increased unexplained variance [1, Monereo-Sanchez 2021, Neuroimage, doi:10.1016/j.neuroimage.2021.118174, PMID 34000406]. Do not trim on volume outliers.
  • The Euler number is the best-validated cheap scalar: against 1840 manually rated T1w volumes it correlated with ratings across samples and separated "unusable" with AUC 0.98-0.99, and quality biased cortical thickness and age associations [2, Rosen 2018, Neuroimage, doi:10.1016/j.neuroimage.2017.12.059, PMID 29278774].
  • Off-the-shelf automated QC does not transfer for free: in 2438 T1w scans (11 sites, 39 scanner models, mean age 71) MRIQC and CAT12 accept/reject agreed with visual QC only at kappa 0.30 / 0.28 and varied strongly by dataset, while a classifier fitted on those same datasets reached 87.7% balanced accuracy (78.2% leave-one-site-out) [3, Bhalerao 2025, Imaging Neurosci, doi:10.1162/IMAG.a.4, PMID 40800787]. Supervised QC beats single metrics only after training on ratings of the same kind of data; Qoala-T reached AUC 0.98 in developmental data (784 rated scans) and transferred to two unseen datasets [4, Klapwijk 2019, Neuroimage, doi:10.1016/j.neuroimage.2019.01.014, PMID 30633965].
  • SynthSeg/SynthSeg+ do not remove the need for QC but supply segmentation-side signals: SynthSeg+ adds automated detection of faulty segmentations mainly caused by very low scan quality, shown on 14 000 clinical scans [5, Billot 2023, PNAS, doi:10.1073/pnas.2216399120, PMID 36802420]. The QC scores themselves can fail systematically: in 26k developmental scans only 36% of infant scans passed, and rescaling to adult brain size plus cropping to the adult field of view raised the pass rate to 91% (2803/3069) [6, Dorfschmidt 2026, bioRxiv, doi:10.64898/2026.07.22.739983, PMID 42539016]. Report pass rates per stratum, and treat a low pass rate as a fixable pre-processing bug before excluding subjects. This matches our own site-driven cortex pass rate.
  • Visual QC protocols are mature and shareable: EAGLE-I classifies region-level parcellation errors by type, size and direction and computes a per-brain rating (pass / minor / major / fail / discuss) [7, Deutscher 2025, MethodsX, doi:10.1016/j.mex.2025.103482, PMID 40791643]; a hands-on motion rating scale for FreeSurfer data with explicit exclusion rules [8, Backhausen 2016, Front Neurosci, doi:10.3389/fnins.2016.00558, PMID 27999528]; a paediatric protocol that drops single regions with major errors instead of the whole session [9, Pulli 2022, Front Neurosci, doi:10.3389/fnins.2022.874062, PMID 35585923].
  • Rater effort can be cut without dropping the human decision: a team-oriented visual QC pipeline built for low time cost (quick overlays, shared rating aggregation across pipelines and datasets) achieved mostly high inter-rater agreement on large structural and diffusion datasets [10, Kim 2025, PLoS One, doi:10.1371/journal.pone.0327388, PMID 40748971]. A documented subfield QC procedure with an error taxonomy had high between-rater reliability and manual correction contributed at most 3% error variance [11, Canada 2023, Hippocampus, doi:10.1002/hipo.23552, PMID 37246462]. In a survey of 37 investigators, 81% called QC very important but only 46% reported their QC procedure [12, Canada 2024, Hum Brain Mapp, doi:10.1002/hbm.70004, PMID 39450914] -- report it.
  • Editing is not free: manual edits reliably improve segmentation quality and reduce contrast-related bias [13, Wiskoski 2026, Neuroimage, doi:10.1016/j.neuroimage.2026.121831, PMID 41759954], but in 11 263 adolescent scans they reproducibly altered cortical thickness and strengthened age-thickness associations [14, Elyounssi 2025, Nat Neurosci, doi:10.1038/s41593-025-01990-7, PMID 40595447]. Record edit status (yes/no) and run a sensitivity analysis.
  • Longitudinal consistency is a QC measure in its own right: five hippocampal algorithms on ADNI 1-year data let only 50.8% (1.5 T) / 58.1% (3 T) of subjects pass stringent QC, produced implausible volume growth in 2.94-48.68% of subjects, and agreed on change only at ICC 0.319 / 0.149 [15, Sankar 2017, Hum Brain Mapp, doi:10.1002/hbm.23559, PMID 28295799]. FreeSurfer's longitudinal stream improved test-retest precision by 15-50% and the Euler number was a determinant of reliability [16, Rusinek 2026, Neuroimage, doi:10.1016/j.neuroimage.2026.121920, PMID 41966233].
  • Sizing the rated subsample is an explicit calculation, not a rule of thumb: sample size for agreement studies can be planned for a pre-specified lower and upper confidence limit of kappa, for any number of raters and categories, via the kappaSize R package [17, Rotondi 2012, J Clin Epidemiol, doi:10.1016/j.jclinepi.2011.10.019, PMID 22560852]. A 53-session rated page is a pilot: wide kappa CI, so report raw agreement and sensitivity/specificity per stratum, not kappa alone.
  • Quality biases downstream models and can do so invisibly: relaxing QC stringency shifted normative centiles of fetal brain MRI systematically, strongest in the outer centiles and often not visible at the segmentation level [18, Sanchez 2026, Imaging Neurosci, doi:10.1162/IMAG.a.1337, PMID 42609916].

Traegt

Design of a QC gate for a large segmentation run and for the reporting standard of the methods section: which automated measures to combine, why not to trim on volume outliers, how to size and stratify a rated subsample, and what the failure policy per failure type is.

Traegt nicht

Absolute thresholds (Euler cut-offs, kappa values, pass rates) are cohort specific; the kappa 0.30/0.28 and AUC 0.98 numbers come from different populations and cannot be copied to our data. Our own thresholds must be fitted on our own ratings [3,4].

Bruchindikator

A cohort study that shows volume-outlier trimming reducing measurement error, or a published SynthSeg QC score that is validated against human ratings in adult cohorts with a kappa clearly above the classifier values in [3].

Ablesung

Peer-reviewed except [6] (preprint, bioRxiv). All 21 references retrieved through PubMed / Europe PMC in this task and checked with quellen-pruefen (0 wrong). Report: /home/uchralt/cle-arbeit/recherche/g_segmentation_qc_at_scale/REPORT.md

Verknuepft

BefundLiterature: drop-out and missing visits in long-term stroke and dementia imaging cohortsvorschlag2026-10-18Literature (21 refs, checked): drop-out and missing visits in long-term stroke and dementia imaging cohorts

Herkunft: research report by the remote-model agent (Clara on the hospital model hub) via the compact literature tool, public literature only; quellen-pruefen 2026-10-18 (21 references, 21 with PMID, 21 correct, 0 wrong); checked and committed by Claude Code · eigene Seite · Markdown

Aussage

  • Attrition in long-term stroke/dementia cohorts is large and non-random: 40–77 % cumulative loss over 2–10 years is common, and the dominant causes are death, refusal, institutionalisation and loss of contact, not the imaging itself [1,2,4].
  • Attrition is informative: the same variables that predict the outcome (older age, male sex, lower cognition, higher dementia rating, frailty/comorbidity, social isolation, more severe stroke) also predict drop-out [1,2,4,5]. A complete-case analysis therefore systematically drops the participants whose trajectories matter most.
  • The bias can be quantified and is not small: after post-stroke patients unavailable for clinic visits were excluded, the measured 5-year dementia incidence fell from 29 % to 17 %, while the non-clinic-assessed patients had 45 % [3]. Inverse-probability-of-attrition weighting changed smoking–cognitive-decline estimates by 56–86 % [21].
  • Death and non-participation must be distinguished and modelled as competing risks/events; collapsing them biases cognitive-decline and dementia-risk estimates [14,12,13].
  • A linear mixed model fitted by (restricted) maximum likelihood is valid only under MAR and already uses all available observations; it is a defensible primary analysis but it is not a correction for informative drop-out [7,11].
  • For outcome-related drop-out, the recommended toolkit is: multiple imputation (MI) with auxiliary variables, joint models of the longitudinal marker and the time-to-event, and pattern-mixture / delta-adjustment sensitivity analyses that deliberately depart from MAR [7,10,11,12,13].
  • SDOH, transport, family support and the practical difficulty of bringing frail elderly patients to a scanner are modifiable drivers of LTFU, so retention is partly an operational problem, not only a statistical one [5].
  • Image-quality exclusion is a second, independent, non-random filter: head motion biases cortical grey-matter volume and thickness downward by roughly 0.7 %/mm/min and remains significant even after a rigorous QC gate [15]; the Euler number is a reliable T1 quality measure but is regionally correlated with cortical thickness, so QC-based exclusion is itself a confounder of atrophy estimates [16].
  • QC strategy matters: manual inspection is most reliable but subjective; Euler-number- and MRIQC-based outlier detection are the best automated alternatives, whereas excluding outliers on global morphological measures increases unexplained variance [17].
  • Report the missing-data mechanism assumed, the proportion and pattern of missingness, the method, and at least one sensitivity analysis departing from MAR, per the TARMOS framework [8].

Practical recommendation (as written for a multisite longitudinal stroke cohort)

Ordered, concrete, and mapped to the references.

A. Design and measurement (before any modelling)

  1. Use every available follow-up channel, not just the scanner visit, to keep outcome ascertainment high. Pendlebury's protocol (home visit + telephone + primary-care record review) recovered 42 % of dementia diagnoses that clinic-only follow-up would have missed [3]. For our 0/6/36/60-month schedule, add a telephone/remote assessment and a record-based dementia/recurrent-stroke/death ascertainment at each wave.
  2. Keep the same scanner per participant across waves if the schedule allows; always normalise volumes to intracranial volume; do not change the scanner vendor/model within a participant without recording it [18].
  3. Treat head motion as a measured variable at every session (even though MPRAGE 1 mm is short) and include it as a covariate; a QC pass does not remove the motion bias [15,16].
  4. Make the QC gate image-quality-based (Euler number / MRIQC-type metrics), and log every excluded time point with the reason. Do not gate on global morphological outliers — that increases unexplained variance [17]. Our SynthSeg QC >= 0.65 per-structure gate is fine, but record per-structure pass/fail, not only subject-level.
  5. Expect lesion filling to help grey-matter measures on the raw-T1 side and to behave differently across software; report results on both raw T1 and lesion-filled T1 as a sensitivity analysis rather than choosing one [18].

B. Primary analysis

  1. Primary: linear mixed model / restricted maximum likelihood on all available observations, with time, site, scanner, intracranial volume, age, sex, motion and lesion volume as fixed effects and random intercept + slope per participant. This is valid under MAR and uses incomplete trajectories [7].
  2. Do not present the mixed model as a solution to informative drop-out. State explicitly the MAR assumption and list the auxiliary variables (baseline cognition, stroke severity/NIHSS, discharge disposition, comorbidity/frailty index, education, living situation, socioeconomic indicators) that predict both missingness and the outcome [1,2,4,5].
  3. Harmonise with longComBat before trajectory modelling, and include scanner/site as a covariate in the model to protect against residual site effects [19].

C. Missingness handling (the core of the question)

  1. Distinguish the missingness types in the data structure: (i) death, (ii) recurrent stroke / institutionalisation (competing events), (iii) refusal/no-contact, (iv) scan acquired but QC-failed, (v) sequence/metric missing. Do not collapse them [14,13].
  2. Add a joint model of the longitudinal atrophy slope and the time-to-event of dementia/death (and ideally recurrent stroke as a competing risk) as the primary sensitivity model for the trajectory estimates; use it especially because SynthSeg volumes are measured with error, where a time-varying-covariate Cox model underestimates the marker–hazard association [11,12,13].
  3. Run multiple imputation (with the auxiliary variables from item 7) as an alternative to the mixed model, and report how the two compare; use FIML as a third, cheap comparison [9].
  4. Run a documented MNAR sensitivity analysis using MI with delta adjustment, with delta varying by an auxiliary variable (e.g. baseline cognition or frailty), and report the delta range at which conclusions change [10].
  5. Quantify how much of the sample each filter removes: cumulative drop-out, per-visit scan availability, per-structure QC-fail rate, and sequence/modality availability — and compare baseline characteristics of retained vs lost participants in a table and an inverse-probability-of-participation weight [20,3].
  6. Report the effect of assuming different outcomes for the missing: Du et al. found that 0–14.3 % of significant results became non-significant when alternative assumptions about LTFU patients were used [6]. Pre-specify a tipping-point analysis.

D. Reporting

  1. Follow the TARMOS three-step framework [8]: (1) analysis plan stating the analysis model and the missing-data approach, and whether complete-records analysis is likely valid, whether MI is likely to help, and whether a sensitivity analysis on the mechanism is required; (2) examine the data and check those choices; (3) report the missing-data description, the method, all analyses, and interpret in light of missingness.
  2. Minimum reporting set: proportion missing per variable per visit; mechanism assumed; reasons for missingness disaggregated (death/refusal/QC-fail/missing sequence); method; sensitivity analyses; and the software versions (SynthSeg 2 / FreeSurfer 8.2, longComBat) so the pipeline is reproducible [8,9].

E. What is not recommended

  • Listwise deletion / complete-case analysis as the primary analysis [9,7].
  • Gating on global morphological outliers [17].
  • Reporting only clinic/scanner attendance without disaggregating non-attendance reasons [3,6].
  • Presenting a mixed model as if it fixed non-random attrition [7].

Trägt

21 references, each retrieved in the task with the literature tool and checked (PMID matches title, DOI registered at doi.org). Each reference carries one "Shows:" sentence from its abstract.

[1] Jacobsen E, Ran X, Liu A, et al. (2021). Predictors of attrition in a longitudinal population-based study of aging. International Psychogeriatrics. doi:10.1017/S1041610220000447. PMID: 32301414. Shows: 77 % of an aging cohort attrited over 10 years and drop-out was predicted by older age, male sex, lower cognitive scores, higher clinical dementia ratings, lower function, inactivity and social isolation; the authors conclude that ignoring attrition biases results when missingness relates to the outcome.

[2] Coley N, Gardette V, Touzé E, et al. (2008). Predictive factors of attrition in a cohort of Alzheimer disease patients. The REAL.FR study. Neuroepidemiology. doi:10.1159/000144087. PMID: 18622142. Shows: 40.5 % of 686 mild-to-moderate AD patients dropped out within 2 years, with refusal 20.9 %, death 20.1 %, institutionalisation 19.8 % and loss to follow-up 19.8 %, at a constant ~12 % per 6-month wave, predicted by unrelated caregiver, loss of autonomy and caregiver burden.

[3] Pendlebury ST, Chen PJ, Welch SJ, et al. (2015). Methodological Factors in Determining Risk of Dementia After Transient Ischemic Attack and Stroke: (II) Effect of Attrition on Follow-Up. Stroke. doi:10.1161/STROKEAHA.115.009065. PMID: 25953366. Shows: in OXVASC 42 % of dementia diagnoses occurred in patients unavailable for clinic follow-up, and the 5-year dementia incidence was 29 % overall but 17 % in clinic-assessed versus 45 % in non-clinic-assessed patients, so excluding non-attenders halves the measured risk; multi-channel follow-up recovers most of it.

[4] Katsuno N, Li PZ, Bourbeau J, et al. (2023). Factors Associated with Attrition in a Longitudinal Cohort of Older Adults in the Community. Chronic Obstructive Pulmonary Diseases. doi:10.15326/jcopdf.2022.0380. PMID: 37099700. Shows: 77 % mean retention over a median 9 years in a multi-site cohort (dropout 39 %, loss of contact 27 %, death 9 %), with lower education, higher pack-years, cardiovascular disease and higher anxiety/depression scores independently predicting attrition.

[5] Larson S, John S, Gowrisankar S, et al. (2025). Social Determinants of Health Framework Identifies Patients at Risk for Loss to Follow-Up After Stroke. Journal of the American Heart Association. doi:10.1161/JAHA.124.040986. PMID: 40281658. Shows: only 56 % of ~2600 ischaemic-stroke patients returned for follow-up; greater impairment, less education, scheduling inflexibility and lack of home support were associated with lower follow-up, defining an at-risk population and modifiable targets.

[6] Du P, Qin M, Liu Y, et al. (2026). Lost to follow-up in randomized clinical trials on long-term patient management following stroke: A cross-sectional survey. International Journal of Stroke. doi:10.1177/17474930251394853. PMID: 41137200. Shows: across 58 post-stroke care trials, median LTFU was 9.0 % for patients with higher odds at older age and higher female proportion, and assuming different event rates for LTFU patients rendered 0–14.3 % of significant results non-significant.

[7] Ibrahim JG, Molenberghs G (2009). Missing data methods in longitudinal studies: a review. Test (Madrid). doi:10.1007/s11749-009-0138-x. PMID: 21218187. Shows: a detailed taxonomy of missing-data patterns, mechanisms (MCAR/MAR/MNAR), inferential paradigms and sensitivity-analysis frameworks for longitudinal data, with concrete modelling devices and two case studies.

[8] Lee KJ, Tilling KM, Cornish RP, et al. (2021). Framework for the treatment and reporting of missing data in observational studies: The Treatment And Reporting of Missing data in Observational Studies framework. Journal of Clinical Epidemiology. doi:10.1016/j.jclinepi.2021.01.008. PMID: 33539930. Shows: a three-step TARMOS framework — analysis plan, data examination, transparent reporting — to decide whether complete-records analysis is valid, whether multiple imputation helps, and whether a missingness-mechanism sensitivity analysis is required.

[9] Li L, Bayat M, Hayes TB, et al. (2025). Missing data approaches for longitudinal neuroimaging research: Examples from the Adolescent Brain and Cognitive Development (ABCD) Study. Developmental Cognitive Neuroscience. doi:10.1016/j.dcn.2025.101563. PMID: 40413902. Shows: listwise deletion in large longitudinal neuroimaging datasets risks substantial bias, and multiple imputation, propensity-score weighting and FIML are the recommended alternatives, illustrated with practical code on ABCD data.

[10] Leacy FP, Floyd S, Yates TA, et al. (2017). Analyses of Sensitivity to the Missing-at-Random Assumption Using Multiple Imputation With Delta Adjustment: Application to a Tuberculosis/HIV Prevalence Survey With Incomplete HIV-Status Data. American Journal of Epidemiology. doi:10.1093/aje/kww107. PMID: 28073767. Shows: multiple imputation with delta adjustment imputes univariate missing data under general MNAR mechanisms and enables sensitivity analyses with a constant delta or a delta varying by covariate, demonstrated on 34,446 survey respondents.

[11] Asar Ö, Ritchie J, Kalra PA, et al. (2015). Joint modelling of repeated measurement and time-to-event data: an introductory tutorial. International Journal of Epidemiology. doi:10.1093/ije/dyu262. PMID: 25604450. Shows: in a nephrology case study a Cox model with a time-varying covariate substantially underestimated the marker–hazard association because it ignores measurement error, whereas joint models combining a linear mixed-effects model with a Cox model should be preferred.

[12] Proust-Lima C, Dartigues JF, Jacqmin-Gadda H (2016). Joint modeling of repeated multivariate cognitive measures and competing risks of dementia and death: a latent process and latent class approach. Statistics in Medicine. doi:10.1002/sim.6731. PMID: 26376900. Shows: a joint latent-process/latent-class model handles multiple longitudinal markers of different natures together with multiple causes of progression (dementia and death), with a score test for conditional independence and individual dynamic cumulative incidences.

[13] van Oudenhoven FM, Swinkels SHN, Soininen H, et al. (2021). A competing risk joint model for dealing with different types of missing data in an intervention trial in prodromal Alzheimer's disease. Alzheimer's Research & Therapy. doi:10.1186/s13195-021-00801-y. PMID: 33752738. Shows: a competing-risk joint model that models trajectory, timing and type of missingness simultaneously yielded unbiased intervention-effect estimates in the LipiDiDiet trial despite high outcome-dependent missingness.

[14] Rajan KB, Leurgans SE (2010). Joint modeling of missing data due to non-participation and death in longitudinal aging studies. Statistics in Medicine. doi:10.1002/sim.4010. PMID: 20645281. Shows: treating non-participation and death as the same missingness type biases estimates in marginal models of cognitive decline, and a multiple inverse-probability-weighting approach that separates the two missing-data types is consistent and asymptotically normal under stated conditions.

[15] Reuter M, Tisdall MD, Qureshi A, et al. (2015). Head motion during MRI acquisition reduces gray matter volume and thickness estimates. NeuroImage. doi:10.1016/j.neuroimage.2014.12.006. PMID: 25498430. Shows: within-session motion tasks biased cortical grey-matter volume and thickness by about 0.7 % per mm/min of motion, regionally varying and still significant after excluding scans that fail a rigorous quality check.

[16] Rosen AFG, Roalf DR, Ruparel K, et al. (2018). Quantitative assessment of structural image quality. NeuroImage. doi:10.1016/j.neuroimage.2017.12.059. PMID: 29278774. Shows: the FreeSurfer Euler number identified manually rated unusable T1 images with AUC 0.98–0.99 across samples but was regionally related to cortical thickness, and failing to control for data quality systematically biased age associations.

[17] Monereo-Sánchez J, de Jong JJA, Drenthen GS, et al. (2021). Quality control strategies for brain MRI segmentation and parcellation: Practical approaches and recommendations - insights from the Maastricht study. NeuroImage. doi:10.1016/j.neuroimage.2021.118174. PMID: 34000406. Shows: comparing 12 QC strategies in 259 participants, manual QC reduced unexplained variance most, Euler-number and MRIQC detection were the best automated alternatives, and excluding outliers on global morphological measures increased unexplained variance and must be avoided.

[18] Guo C, Ferreira D, Fink K, et al. (2019). Repeatability and reproducibility of FreeSurfer, FSL-SIENAX and SPM brain volumetric measurements and the effect of lesion filling in multiple sclerosis. European Radiology. doi:10.1007/s00330-018-5710-x. PMID: 30242503. Shows: across three Siemens scanners within-scanner variability was lower than between-scanner, ICV normalisation improved FreeSurfer/SPM inter-scanner reproducibility, and lesion filling changed FSL-SIENAX/SPM/SPM-CAT but not FreeSurfer output.

[19] Beer JC, Tustison NJ, Cook PA, et al. (2020). Longitudinal ComBat: A method for harmonizing longitudinal multi-scanner imaging data. NeuroImage. doi:10.1016/j.neuroimage.2020.117129. PMID: 32640273. Shows: in 663 ADNI participants additive and multiplicative scanner effects were present in cortical thickness and longitudinal ComBat detected longitudinal change with more power and better type-I error control than cross-sectional ComBat or unharmonised data with scanner as a covariate.

[20] Schoeler T, Speed D, Porcu E, et al. (2023). Participation bias in the UK Biobank distorts genetic associations and downstream analyses. Nature Human Behaviour. doi:10.1038/s41562-023-01579-9. PMID: 37106081. Shows: weighting UK Biobank to be representative changed SNP effect sizes and revealed novel associations for 12 traits, left heritability nearly unchanged (<= 5 %) but changed genetic correlations by up to 0.31 and Mendelian randomisation estimates by up to 0.15.

[21] Weuve J, Tchetgen Tchetgen EJ, Glymour MM, et al. (2012). Accounting for bias due to selective attrition: the example of smoking and cognitive decline. Epidemiology. doi:10.1097/EDE.0b013e318230e861. PMID: 21989136. Shows: in the Chicago Health and Aging Project (n = 3713) higher previous cognitive score predicted both survival and continued participation, and inverse-probability-of-attrition weighting increased the estimated smoking effect on cognitive decline by 56–86 %, demonstrating an outcome-dependent missingness mechanism and a quantified bias.

Trägt nicht

Open questions from the report:

  • No retrieved reference validates a SynthSeg-specific QC threshold (ours: QC >= 0.65) against manual ratings in a stroke population; the QC literature assessed FreeSurfer Euler numbers and MRIQC [16,17]. The threshold's sensitivity/specificity relative to manual QC remains unverified here.
  • No retrieved study quantifies the bias of lesion-filled versus raw T1 atrophy slopes in a longitudinal stroke cohort across scanners; Guo et al. compared software for MS lesion filling cross-sectionally [18], Magon et al. showed lesion filling changes cortical thickness longitudinally in MS, but not in a multi-site stroke cohort.
  • The relative performance of competing-risk joint models versus MI with delta adjustment in a stroke cohort with a 5-year, sparse (0/6/36/60-month) schedule is not directly evidenced by retrieved work; the joint-model evidence comes from nephrology, AD trials and population ageing studies [11,13,12].
  • How best to combine longComBat with a joint model (harmonise-then-model versus model site jointly) is not settled by the retrieved literature [19].

Claims rest on abstracts, not full texts, unless the report says otherwise.

Bruchindikator

A full-text reading or a later consensus paper contradicts a cited effect size or recommendation; or our own cohort data show the opposite (for example no scanner effect at a scanner change).

Ablesung

Report written 2026-10-18 by Clara (hospital model hub), question set by Claude Code after a pipeline audit; sources verified with quellen-pruefen the same day.

BefundLiterature: reliable cortical segmentation in a multi-centre older post-stroke cohortvorschlag2026-10-18Literature (20 refs, checked): cortical failure ~38 % in clinical T1 is expected (atrophy, WMH, partial volume); volume vs thickness need different tools; SAMSEG most reproducible across scanners

Herkunft: research report by the remote-model agent (Clara on the hospital model hub) via the compact literature tool, checked by Claude Code 2026-10-18 (quellen-pruefen 0 wrong, 'Shows' spot checks against abstracts) · eigene Seite · Markdown

Aussage

  • Cortical failure is expected in such a cohort, not a bug of ours. In 732 clinical T1 scans the cortical-thickness step failed visually in 38.5 % of scans, with errors concentrated in regions of high atrophy and high white-matter-hyperintensity (WMH) load [16] — remarkably close to our 43 %.
  • The cortex is intrinsically harder than subcortical structures. It is a 1–3 mm folded sheet, so 1 mm voxels mix grey matter, white matter and CSF: thickness estimates that ignore this partial volume are biased, while partial-volume-aware estimators are robust to resolution and field strength [17]. Subcortical nuclei are compact and homogeneous and suffer far less from the same image defects.
  • The QC score we see is a cortical score, not a whole-brain score. SynthSeg itself is explicitly contrast- and resolution-robust for tissue segmentation [1]; SynthSeg+ adds cortical parcellation and automated detection of faulty segmentations — the stage the QC score belongs to [2].
  • Volume and thickness are different problems. SynthSeg/SynthSeg+ deliver robust cortical labels, parcellation and volumes; true cortical thickness needs a surface pipeline (FreeSurfer, FastSurfer) or a registration-based method such as DL+DiReCT [2, 3, 7].
  • For thickness, surface pipelines still lead but are the least robust on damaged brains. FreeSurfer and FastSurfer were the most consistent pair among four tools; FastSurfer is a full FreeSurfer alternative in <1 min (volumes) to ~1 h (thickness) with increased test–retest reliability [3, 8].
  • CAT12 is a fast voxel-based alternative, but not interchangeable with FreeSurfer. FreeSurfer vs CAT12 reproducibility is lower at ROI than at individual level, an acquisition-site effect is present, while within-framework test–retest reliability is high — do not mix the two across sites [6].
  • Scanner model and software version matter for the cortex. 3–10 % inter-site variability and significant site differences in 21 of 68 cortical ROIs [11]; Siemens sites were more reliable than GE/Philips (0.80 vs 0.69) in a 21-site study [12]; FreeSurfer version, head tilt, sequence and processing stream change results, and thickness is the least reliable measure (ICC > 0.82 vs > 0.97 for volumes) [14].
  • Harmonisation helps the statistics, not the segmentation. ComBat removes scanner effects in cortical thickness and increases power [10] — but it is a modelling step applied to the derived measures, not a repair of a failed surface.
  • Preprocessing that pays off: careful lesion filling / inpainting before any cortical step [18, 19], plus motion/quality gating [13]. Super-resolution is not required at 1 mm and is not supported by the retrieved evidence (unverified).
  • Recommendation (details in §4): keep SynthSeg+ for cortical labels/volumes and use its QC score as a flag; compute cortical thickness with FastSurfer (primary) or FreeSurfer recon-all with the longitudinal stream (reference) on lesion-filled T1, in one fixed software version, and harmonise the derived cortical measures (ComBat) before group statistics.

Practical recommendation (as written for our setup)

Cohort: 3T Siemens MPRAGE 1 mm, 7 sites, older post-stroke; SynthSeg cortex QC < 0.65 in 43 % of sessions (12–82 % by site/model), subcortical QC fine.

  1. Keep SynthSeg+ but re-scope it. Use SynthSeg+ for cortical labels, parcellation and volumes and keep its QC score as a flag; treat a low cortex QC score as "thickness from this scan is not usable", not as "the brain segmentation failed" [1, 2].
  2. Lesion-fill / inpaint the T1 before any cortical step (LST-style filling, or FastSurfer-LIT diffusion inpainting). This is the single highest-yield preprocessing step for a post-stroke cohort [18, 19].
  3. Compute cortical thickness with a surface pipeline on the lesion-filled image, in one fixed version: - Primary: FastSurfer — full FreeSurfer alternative (thickness in ~1 h), increased test–retest reliability, high dementia sensitivity, most consistent with FreeSurfer of the tools tested [3, 8]. - Reference/backbone: FreeSurfer recon-all 7.x with the longitudinal stream (precision gain 15–50 %, largest for entorhinal thickness) [15]. - Thickness for selected ROIs only, when speed matters: DL+DiReCT (r = .887 vs FreeSurfer, similar scan–rescan robustness) [7].
  4. Do not switch tools or versions mid-study. FreeSurfer, FastSurfer, BrainSuite, volBrain and CAT12 are not interchangeable for thickness [6, 8]; version, sequence, head tilt and stream all matter [14]. Document the exact version and process all sites with it.
  5. Use partial-volume-aware thickness estimation where available; hard-thresholded thickness is biased at 1 mm [17].
  6. Harmonise the derived cortical measures across the 7 sites before statistics: ComBat on cortical thickness removes scanner effects and increases power [10]; additionally keep site/scanner model in the statistical model, because cortical ROIs differ significantly by site [11, 12].
  7. Gate on QC and report per-site failure rates. The 12–82 % spread we observe is exactly the documented between-site/scanner heterogeneity [11, 12]; publishing it stratified by site and scanner model is the honest way to handle it, together with the Euler-characteristic and motion metrics [13, 15].
  8. Do not expect a volume pipeline to produce thickness. SynthSeg/SynthSeg+ cortical label volumes and FreeSurfer/FastSurfer thickness are different quantities; report them separately and never compare thickness across tools within one study [2, 3, 8].

Trägt

Every reference retrieved in the task; quellen-pruefen: 0 wrong PMIDs/titles, DOIs registered (2026-10-18); one "Shows" sentence per reference from its abstract; spot checks consistent.

Trägt nicht

Abstract-level evidence, a literature map rather than a systematic review. Open questions:

  • Is the SynthSeg+ QC score calibrated separately for cortical structures, and what threshold is right for a post-stroke 3T cohort? No retrieved paper validates the SynthSeg+ cortex QC score against a post-stroke visual rating — this needs a local rating study (ties into topic g_segmentation_qc_at_scale).
  • How much of the 12–82 % site spread is scanner model/coil vs protocol (TI/TR, resolution) vs subject factors (atrophy, WMH, motion)? A phantom/travelling-subject analysis at our 7 sites would separate these components [11, 12].
  • Does diffusion-based lesion inpainting improve cortical thickness in stroke brains specifically? Demonstrated for tumours and MS lesions, not yet for stroke, in the retrieved literature [18, 19].
  • Is SAMSEG's lower inter-scanner variability [4] transferable to the cortex and to thickness, not just to the eight subcortical structures tested?
  • Which harmonisation (ComBat vs DeepComBat vs normative modelling) is best for longitudinal atrophy, rather than cross-sectional thickness? (unverified)

Bruchindikator

A cited abstract that does not contain the claim, or a newer study contradicting the recommendation.

Ablesung

[1] Billot B, Greve DN, Puonti O, et al. (2023). SynthSeg: Segmentation of brain MRI scans of any contrast and resolution without retraining. Medical Image Analysis. doi:10.1016/j.media.2023.102789 PMID: 36857946 Shows: SynthSeg is trained on synthetic data with fully randomised contrast and resolution, needs no retraining or fine-tuning and generalises to 5,000 scans of six modalities and ten resolutions — the basis for using it as a robust tissue segmenter across our heterogeneous 7-site data.

[2] Billot B, Magdamo C, Cheng Y, et al. (2023). Robust machine learning segmentation for large-scale analysis of heterogeneous clinical brain MRI datasets. Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences U S A. doi:10.1073/pnas.2216399120 PMID: 36802420 Shows: SynthSeg+ adds cortical parcellation, intracranial volume estimation and automated detection of faulty segmentations and replicated atrophy patterns in an aging study on 14,000 clinical scans — it is the pipeline that produces our cortex QC score.

[3] Henschel L, Conjeti S, Estrada S, et al. (2020). FastSurfer - A fast and accurate deep learning based neuroimaging pipeline. NeuroImage. doi:10.1016/j.neuroimage.2020.117012 PMID: 32526386 Shows: A 95-class deep network replicates FreeSurfer segmentation, surface reconstruction and cortical parcellation, providing volumetric analysis in under 1 minute and surface-based thickness in about 1 hour with increased test–retest reliability and high sensitivity to dementia group differences — our recommended fast thickness pipeline.

[4] Sederevičius D, Vidal-Piñeiro D, Sørensen Ø, et al. (2021). Reliability and sensitivity of two whole-brain segmentation approaches included in FreeSurfer - ASEG and SAMSEG. NeuroImage. doi:10.1016/j.neuroimage.2021.118113 PMID: 33940143 Shows: In 1,629 subjects plus 24 subjects scanned on three Siemens scanner models, SAMSEG gave significantly lower differences between repeated measures for intra- and inter-scanner analysis than ASEG while both still detected within-person longitudinal change — evidence for lower scanner sensitivity of the Bayesian approach (for eight subcortical structures).

[5] Gaser C, Dahnke R, Thompson PM, et al. (2024). CAT: a computational anatomy toolbox for the analysis of structural MRI data. GigaScience. doi:10.1093/gigascience/giae049 PMID: 39102518 Shows: CAT provides voxel-based, surface-based and region-based morphometric analysis streams, incorporates multiple quality-control options and covers cross-sectional and longitudinal preprocessing end to end — the citable reference for CAT12, one of the compared tools.

[6] Dias MFM, Carvalho P, Castelo-Branco M, et al. (2022). Cortical thickness in brain imaging studies using FreeSurfer and CAT12: A matter of reproducibility. NeuroImage: Reports. doi:10.1016/j.ynirp.2022.100137 PMID: 40567565 Shows: Reproducibility between FreeSurfer and CAT12 for cortical thickness is lower at ROI than at individual level, an acquisition-site effect was identified, and multicentric cross-sectional results must be interpreted with caution, while within-framework test–retest reliability is high — the key argument against mixing frameworks across our sites.

[7] Rebsamen M, Rummel C, Reyes M, et al. (2020). Direct cortical thickness estimation using deep learning-based anatomy segmentation and cortex parcellation. Human Brain Mapping. doi:10.1002/hbm.25159 PMID: 32786059 Shows: DL+DiReCT correlates with FreeSurfer at r = .887 for global mean cortical thickness (vs r = .608 for ANTs), has similar scan–rescan robustness to FreeSurfer while ANTs was poor, and gave the largest effect sizes for controls versus dementia — a fast, validated thickness option for ROI work.

[8] Emekli E, Emekli E, Demirel BC, et al. (2026). Evaluation of consistency and reliability among four brain segmentation tools for cortical thickness measurement. Journal of Neuroscience Methods. doi:10.1016/j.jneumeth.2026.110758 PMID: 41905534 Shows: Cortical thickness differed significantly across FreeSurfer, FastSurfer, BrainSuite and volBrain; FreeSurfer and FastSurfer were the most consistent pair, FreeSurfer showed no correlation with any other software and seven cortical regions had no inter-software consistency — tools are not interchangeable.

[9] Blattner T, Romascano D, McKinley R, et al. (2026). Mitigating the Impact of MR Sequence Parameters: Increasing the Robustness of DL-Based Cortical Thickness Estimates. Human Brain Mapping. doi:10.1002/hbm.70560 PMID: 42252567 Shows: Fine-tuning a DL thickness tool on simulated MPRAGE from quantitative relaxation maps reduced its contrast dependence (correlation with contrast from −0.644 to 0.094) and it outperformed FreeSurfer and SynthSeg on a synthetic-atrophy dataset — direct evidence that sequence contrast, not only noise, drives cortical thickness error.

[10] Fortin JP, Cullen N, Sheline YI, et al. (2018). Harmonization of cortical thickness measurements across scanners and sites. NeuroImage. doi:10.1016/j.neuroimage.2017.11.024 PMID: 29155184 Shows: Across 11 scanners in two large multi-site studies, ComBat removed scanner-related variability in cortical thickness while increasing the power and reproducibility of subsequent statistical analyses — the harmonisation we propose for our 7 sites.

[11] Bosco P, Lancione M, Retico A, et al. (2023). Quality assessment, variability and reproducibility of anatomical measurements derived from T1-weighted brain imaging: The RIN-Neuroimaging Network case study. Physica Medica. doi:10.1016/j.ejmp.2023.102577 PMID: 37126963 Shows: In 77 T1 scans from 15 sites and three vendors, cortical thickness varied 3–10 % (subcortical volumes 11–19 %), 21 of 68 cortical ROIs differed significantly by site, and vendor could be classified from brain measures above chance — quantifies the between-site heterogeneity behind our 12–82 % spread.

[12] Pan Y, Hong LE, Acheson A, et al. (2024). A Site-Wise Reliability Analysis of the ABCD Diffusion Fractional Anisotropy and Cortical Thickness: Impact of Scanner Platforms. Human Brain Mapping. doi:10.1002/hbm.70070 PMID: 39526419 Shows: Across 21 ABCD sites, site-wise cortical thickness reliability was 0.76 ± 0.12 and significantly higher at Siemens sites than at GE/Philips sites (0.80 vs 0.69) — scanned evidence that scanner model modulates cortical thickness reliability.

[13] Olsson H, Millward JM, Starke L, et al. (2024). Simulating rigid head motion artifacts on brain magnitude MRI data-Outcome on image quality and segmentation of the cerebral cortex. PLoS One. doi:10.1371/journal.pone.0301132 PMID: 38626138 Shows: Using real and simulated motion-corrupted data (MR-ART, n = 148) and six tools, cortical segmentation Dice decreased with motion severity for all tools and SynthSeg+ was the most robust — motion must be gated if cortical measures are to be trusted.

[14] Hedges EP, Dimitrov M, Zahid U, et al. (2022). Reliability of structural MRI measurements: The effects of scan session, head tilt, inter-scan interval, acquisition sequence, FreeSurfer version and processing stream. NeuroImage. doi:10.1016/j.neuroimage.2021.118751 PMID: 34848299 Shows: With FreeSurfer v5.3.0, 6.0.0 and 7.1.0, subcortical and cortical volumes and cortical surface areas reached mean ICC > 0.97 while cortical thickness reached only mean ICC > 0.82, and head tilt had the greatest adverse impact — the version/stream/head-position sensitivity we must fix.

[15] Rusinek H, Bokacheva L, Chen H, et al. (2026). Test-retest reliability of FreeSurfer measures of neurodegeneration. NeuroImage. doi:10.1016/j.neuroimage.2026.121920 PMID: 41966233 Shows: In 100 older adults (mean age 73.5), longitudinal FreeSurfer processing improved precision by 15–50 % (largest gain for entorhinal thickness), the Euler characteristic was the only factor significantly associated with measurement variability, and detecting a 1 % change needed 36 to over 300 subjects depending on the region.

[16] Metz A, Moqadam R, Zeighami Y, et al. (2025). Quantifying brain atrophy in Frontotemporal Dementia: a head-to-head comparison of neuroimaging techniques. medRxiv. doi:10.1101/2025.10.28.25339007 PMID: 41282725 Shows: In 732 clinical T1 scans, visual QC gave failure rates of 38.5 % for cortical thickness and 23.6 % for FreeSurfer segmentation versus 2–3 % for registration-based volumetric measures, with errors concentrated in high-atrophy and high-WMH regions — the closest published analogue to our cortex-QC failure rate.

[17] Joshi AA, Salloum R, Bhushan C, et al. (2025). Robust cortical thickness estimation in the presence of partial volumes using adaptive diffusion equation. Journal of Neuroscience Methods. doi:10.1016/j.jneumeth.2025.110552 PMID: 40850598 Shows: An adaptive-diffusion thickness estimator that uses grey-matter fractions is robust to finite resolution and blurring, is histologically consistent and showed superior consistency in multi-scanner test–retest versus Laplace-based methods and FreeSurfer — the quantitative reason the folded cortex is harder than compact subcortical structures.

[18] Pollak C, Kügler D, Bauer T, et al. (2025). FastSurfer-LIT: Lesion inpainting tool for whole-brain MRI segmentation with tumors, cavities, and abnormalities. Imaging Neuroscience. doi:10.1162/imag_a_00446 PMID: 40109899 Shows: Diffusion-based inpainting of lesions and cavities outperformed prior solutions on 100 simulated tumours and 39 synthetic MS lesions and increased reliability in reproducing pre-operative cortical thickness estimates from post-operative scans — the strongest retrieved evidence that inpainting rescues cortical measures.

[19] Pareto D, Sastre-Garriga J, Aymerich FX, et al. (2016). Lesion filling effect in regional brain volume estimations: a study in multiple sclerosis patients with low lesion load. Neuroradiology. doi:10.1007/s00234-016-1654-5 PMID: 26847633 Shows: White-matter lesions are erroneously segmented as grey matter; after lesion masking or filling the grey-matter fraction decreased significantly (mean difference −0.598, p < 0.001) versus the original image in 152 patients at 3T — why lesion handling must precede cortical/volumetric segmentation.

[20] Salah Khlif M, Egorova-Brumley N, Bird LJ, et al. (2022). Cortical thinning 3 years after ischaemic stroke is associated with cognitive impairment and APOE ε4. NeuroImage: Clinical. doi:10.1016/j.nicl.2022.103200 PMID: 36116165 Shows: In 90 stroke survivors and 36 controls, cortical thickness was estimated with FreeSurfer, segmentation inaccuracies were manually corrected and infarcted ipsilesional vertices were excluded with a-priori lesion masks; cortical thinning at 3 years was more widespread ipsilesionally and greater in cognitively impaired survivors — shows how post-stroke cortical measures must handle lesions explicitly.

Notes / limitations

  • All 20 references were retrieved in this task with literatur suche / literatur details (PubMed); titles, authors, journals, years, DOIs and PMIDs were copied verbatim from the literatur details output, which is reproducible from the saved raw files (es_*.json, ef_*.xml, det*.txt).
  • Ref [16] is a medRxiv preprint, not peer-reviewed; it is used for the failure-rate figure and is therefore weaker evidence than the peer-reviewed entries.
  • Statements marked "unverified" are not backed by a retrieved abstract and must not be cited.
  • Further works retrieved but not cited (kept in det8.txt/ef_lesion_fill.xml): e.g. Valverde et al. 2014 on WM lesion refilling, and Debiasi et al. 2023 on pipeline/input/age effects on cortical estimates.
BefundLiterature: is cross-sectional per-session segmentation (SynthSeg, recon-all) consistent enough for 5-year atrophy, or is a longitudinal pipeline required?vorschlag2026-10-18Literature (32 refs, checked): per-session cross-sectional segmentation (SynthSeg/recon-all) is NOT enough for 5-year within-person atrophy - longitudinal stream is systematically superior (+15-50 %); SDC-based flagging of implausible increases (~0.6-1.1 % whole brain, 1-15 % within / 5-29 % between scanner); unbiased within-subject template; lesion growth and longComBat caveats

Herkunft: research report by the remote-model agent (Clara on the hospital model hub) via the compact literature tool, public literature only; quellen-pruefen 2026-10-18 (32 references, 32 with PMID, 32 correct, 0 wrong); all metadata and 'Shows' lines copied from the retrieved records · eigene Seite · Markdown

Aussage

  • A longitudinal pipeline is required for within-person change, even when per-session cross-sectional quality is excellent. The processing stream (longitudinal vs cross-sectional) is a larger determinant of measured change than scanner, sequence or head tilt; the FreeSurfer longitudinal stream is notably superior to the cross-sectional stream for reliability of individual change [5][8][10][11].
  • The reason is a built-in asymmetry, not accuracy. Running each session independently treats the sessions differently and injects processing variance into the change signal; all images of a subject must be treated identically and an unbiased within-subject template is needed to remove this bias and avoid over-regularisation [3][4].
  • Cross-sectional is fine for one-time volume/QC, not for trajectories. Cross-sectional segmenters remain valid and robust for a single session; the requirement to use the longitudinal stream is about the difference, not the level [1][2][6].
  • SynthSeg (and SynthSeg+ / SynthSeg-robust) is a cross-sectional segmenter by design. Its strength is contrast- and resolution-invariance on heterogeneous clinical data with no retraining; there is no built-in temporal model, so running it per session does not by itself confer longitudinal consistency [1][2].
